B.2.
\Sigma
-
HCOL
operators

There are fourteen
\Sigma
-
HCOL
operators described below. Unless
specified otherwise, they operate on sparse vectors with elements of
type
(\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{fm}}\;\mathrm{fm})
for any

\mathrm{fm}\in\mathrm{Monoid}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}
.

{\mathtt{Embed}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(n\;b\colon\mathbb{N})\;(bc\colon b\,<\,n)\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;1\;n\;s

Takes an element from a single-element input vector and puts it at a
specific index
b
in a sparse vector of given length.

Figure 30.
{\mathtt{Embed}}
dataflow

{\mathtt{Pick}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(n\;b\colon\mathbb{N})\;(bc\colon b<n)\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;n\;1\;s

Selects an element from the input vector at the given index
b
and
returns it as a single element vector.

Figure 31.
{\mathtt{Pick}}
dataflow

{\mathtt{Scatter}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(n\;m\colon\mathbb{N})\;(f\colon\mathtt{index{\_}map}\;n\;m)\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;n\;m\;s

Embedding can be generalized where more than one element can be
embedded at once. The destination selection is controlled by a
user-provided
index mapping function
.

The operator maps elements of the input vector to the elements of the output
according to an index mapping function
f
. The mapping needs to be

injective
but not necessarily
surjective
. That means
the output vector could be sparse.

Figure 32.
{\mathtt{Scatter}}
dataflow

{\mathtt{Gather}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(n\;m\colon\mathbb{N})\;(f\colon\mathtt{index{\_}map}\;m\;n)\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;n\;m\;s

Picking can be generalized where more than one element can be picked
at once. The element selection is controlled by a user-provided

index mapping function

f
.

Figure 33.
{\mathtt{Gather}}
dataflow

{\mathtt{liftM{\_}HOperator}}\;(i\;o\colon\mathbb{N})\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(op\colon\mathtt{avecotr}\;i\rightarrow\mathtt{avector}\;o)\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;i\;o\;s

This operator allows “lifting”
HCOL
operators, so they can be used
in
\Sigma
-
HCOL
expressions. Since
HCOL
operates on dense vectors, the
input vector is first “densified” by dropping structural
flags and replacing sparse values with the
s
. During this operation, structural values become indistinguishable from non-structural values. After applying the
HCOL
operator, the result is
“sparsified” (converted to sparse vector format) by marking all
values as non-structural.

{\mathtt{SHPointwise}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(n\colon\mathbb{N})\;(f\colon\mathbb{I}_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R})\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;n\;n\;s

This is a
\Sigma
-
HCOL
version of the
HPointwise
operator from
HCOL
(see
Section
A.2
). We could not just lift
HPointwise

via
liftM_Hoperator
because we want to preserve structural
flags. However, it can be shown that the two implementations are
equal with respect to
SHOperator_equiv
which only compares
values and ignores flags.

{\mathtt{SHBinOp}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(n\colon\mathbb{N})\;(f\colon\mathbb{I}_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R})\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;(n+n)\;n\;s

This is a
\Sigma
-
HCOL
version of the
HBinOp
operator from
HCOL
(see
Section
A.2
). Just like with
SHPointwise
, can not
just lift the
HBinOp
via the
liftM_Hoperator
because we
want to preserve structural flags. However, it can be shown that the
two implementations are equal with respect to
SHOperator_equiv

which only compares values and ignores flags.

{\mathtt{SHInductor}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(n\colon\mathbb{N})\;(f\colon\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R})\;(z\colon\mathcal{R})\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;1\;1\;s

This is a
\Sigma
-
HCOL
version of the
HInductor
operator from
HCOL
(see
Section
A.2
). The initial value
z
is treated as

non-structural
.

{\mathtt{Apply2Union}}\;(i\;o\colon\mathbb{N})\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(dot\colon\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R})\;(f\;g\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;i\;o\;s)\colon

\mathtt{SHOperator}\;fm\;i\;o\;s

This is a higher-order operator applying two operators to the same input and
combining their results using
Vec2Union
(See
Listing
).

Figure 34.
{\mathtt{Apply2Union}}
dataflow

The additional constraint is that the default value for sparse cells
(
s
) is a fixpoint of the provided binary function
dot
,
defined as
dot(s,s)=s
. This is to assure that
the value of sparse cells is preserved when combining them.

{\mathtt{SafeCast}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(i\;o\colon\mathbb{N})\;(f\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}^{\prime}i\;o\;s)\colon

\mathtt{SHOperator}\;\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}\;i\;o\;s

This is a higher-order operator wrapping another
\Sigma
-
HCOL
operator. While it
does not change the values computed by the wrapped operator, it swaps
the monadic wrapper used to track sparsity properties from

\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}^{\prime}
to

\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}
. As we recall from
Section
3.1.1
, the former does not track
collisions, while the latter does.

{\mathtt{UnSafeCast}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(i\;o\colon\mathbb{N})\;(f\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}i\;o\;s)\colon

\mathtt{SHOperator}\;\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}^{\prime}\;i\;o\;s

Similar to
SafeCast
but with wrappers switched in the opposite
direction, from
\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}
to

\mathtt{Monoid}\_\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{flags}}^{\prime}
.

{\mathtt{SHCompose}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(i1\;o2\;o3\colon\mathbb{N})\;(g\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;o2\;o3\;s)\;(f\colon\mathtt{SHOperator}\;i2\;o2\;s)\colon

\mathtt{SHOperator}\;i1\;o3\;s

This performs a functional composition of operators. The
f
is applied to the
input first. The result is then used as an input of
g
. Sometimes,
we use shortcut notation
(g\circledcirc f)

alluding to the function composition operator
\circ
.

{\mathtt{IReduction}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(i\;o\;n\colon\mathbb{N})\;(dot\colon\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R})\;(F\colon\mathtt{SHOperatorFamily}\;fm\;i\;o\;n\;s)\colon

\mathtt{SHOperator}\;i\;o\;s

This iteratively applies an indexed family
F
of
n
operators to the input and
combines their outputs element-wise using the provided binary function
dot
.
See additional discussion on implementation of this operator in
Section
3.1.9
.

The additional constraint is that the default value for sparse cells
(
s
) is a fixpoint of provided binary function
dot
,
defined as
dot(s,s)=s
. This is to assure that
the value of sparse cells is preserved when combining two of them.

This operator is defined for vectors of
\mathcal{R}_{\theta^{\prime}}
as its intended use
is to computationally combine the results of a family of operators, and it
must not treat combining non-sparse values as errors (collisions).

{\mathtt{IUnion}}\;(s\colon\mathcal{R})\;(i\;o\;n\colon\mathbb{N})\;(dot\colon\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{R})\;(F\colon\mathtt{SHOperatorFamily}\;fm\;i\;o\;n\;s)\colon

\mathtt{SHOperator}\;i\;o\;s

Iteratively applies an indexed family
F
of
n
operators to the input and
combines their outputs element-wise using the provided binary function
dot
.
See additional discussion on implementation of this operator in
Section
3.1.9
.

The additional constraint is that the default value for sparse cells
(
s
) is a fixpoint of the provided binary function
dot
,
defined as
dot(s,s)=s
. This is to assure that
the value of sparse cells is preserved when combining them.

This operator is defined for vectors of
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}
and represents an
abstraction for a parallel loop, combining the results of partial
computations. To allow
loop-level parallelism
in a
well-formed
\Sigma
-
HCOL
expression, partial computations should never
overlap. Such overlapping can not occur as long as the sparsity
patterns of
F
members are disjoint. The collision
tracking built-in in
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}
allows us to prove this.

Appendix C
MSHCOL
language

MSHCOL
language is similar to
\Sigma
-
HCOL
. However, unlike
\Sigma
-
HCOL
operators,

MSHCOL
operators operate on memory blocks rather then sparse vectors.
MSHCOL
is also mixed-embedded in Coq:
MSHCOL
operators are represented with
MSHOperator
record, which contains a function on memory blocks alongside with
two sets that define input and output memory access patterns. See
Section
4.1
for more details.

C.1.
MSHCOL
syntax

MSHCOL
syntax is a subset of Coq’s syntax and is made up of the
following syntactic categories.
\langle
Var
\rangle
represents names for
variables that can be introduced by the

\lambda
-abstraction.
\langle
NatConst
\rangle
and
\langle
CTypeConst
\rangle
represent
literals for values of types
\mathbb{N}
and
\mathcal{R}
respectively, while

\langle
NTypeExpr
\rangle
and
\langle
CTypeExpr
\rangle
represent syntax for expressions
which evaluate to those types.
\langle
CTypeUnFun
\rangle
and

\langle
CTypeBinFun
\rangle
represent unary and binary functions on

\mathcal{R}
.
\langle
CTypeIUnFun
\rangle
and
\langle
CTypeIBinFun
\rangle
are similar to
those but also take additional argument of type
\mathbb{N}
which represents
an index in the input vector.
\langle
NatBinFun
\rangle
represents binary
functions on
\mathbb{N}
.
\langle
VectorConst
\rangle
represents
\mathcal{R}^{n}

constants.
\langle
CoqProof
\rangle
represents Coq proof
terms.
\langle
MSHOperator
\rangle
and
\langle
MSHOperatorFamily
\rangle
represent

MSHCOL
operators and operator families.

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

variable names

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

literals for natural numbers

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{Zero}\;\mid\;\texttt{One}

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{VectorConstBase}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\;\mid\;\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\texttt{,}\;\langle\textrm{VectorConstBase}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{VectorConst}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{[}\langle\textrm{VectorConstBase}\rangle\texttt{]}

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CoqProof}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

Coq proof terms

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\;\mid\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeUnFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeBinFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeIUnFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeIBinFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{Vnth}\;\langle\textrm{VectorConst}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeUnFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{abs}\;\mid\;\texttt{fun}\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\texttt{=>}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeBinFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{plus}\;\mid\;\texttt{sub}\;\mid\;\texttt{mul}\;\mid\;\texttt{min}\;\mid\;\texttt{max}\;\mid\;\texttt{lt}\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{fun}\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\texttt{=>}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeIUnFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{fun}\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\texttt{=>}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeIBinFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{fun}\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\texttt{=>}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\;\mid\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\mid\;\langle\textrm{NatBinFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NatBinFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{add}\;\mid\;\texttt{sub}\;\mid\;\texttt{mul}\;\mid\;\texttt{fun}\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\texttt{=>}\;\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{MSHOperator}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{MSHEmbed}\;\langle\textrm{CoqProof}\rangle\;\mid\;\texttt{MSHPick}\;\langle\textrm{CoqProof}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{MSHPointwise}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeIUnFun}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{MSHBinOp}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeIUnFun}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{MSHInductor}\;\langle\textrm{NatExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeBinFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{MApply2Union}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeBinFun}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\textrm{MSHOperator}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{MSHOperator}\rangle

\displaystyle\texttt{MSHCompose}\;\langle\textrm{MSHOperator}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{MSHOperator}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{MSHIReduction}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeBinFun}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\textrm{MSHOperatorFamily}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{MSHIUnion}\;\langle\textrm{MSHOperatorFamily}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{MSHOperatorFamily}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{fun}\;\langle\textrm{Var}\rangle\;\texttt{=>}\;\langle\textrm{MSHOperator}\rangle

C.2.
MSHCOL
operators

Recall from Section
4.1
that there is a 1-to-1 correspondence
between
MSHCOL
operators and operators from the final subset of
\Sigma
-
HCOL
with the
main difference in the input and output container data types:
MSHCOL
uses
memory blocks, where
\Sigma
-
HCOL
uses sparse vectors.

Appendix D
DHCOL
language family

DHCOL
is a language family, parametrised by
CType.t
and
NType.t

types which are used to represent data values and integer values respectively.
Different instantiations of this parametrisation result in different languages
of
DHCOL
family. In HELIX we define two such languages:
RHCOL
and
FHCOL
.

Unlike all the previous languages in HELIX compilation chain,
DHCOL
languages
are imperative.
DHCOL
operators can not only compute values, but also modify
the memory state.
DHCOL
languages are deep-embedded in Coq:
DHCOL
operators
are represented as constructors of the
DSHOperator
inductive type, wich is
used to represent an abstract syntax tree of a
DHCOL
program. See
Section
5
for more details.

D.1.
DHCOL
syntax

In this section, we present
DHCOL
syntax using BNF notation. As
DHCOL
language
family is parametrized by
CType.t
and
NType.t
types,
DHCOL
syntax
depends on the syntax for literals used to represent values of those types.

DHCOL
syntax is made up of the following syntactic categories.
\langle
NatConst
\rangle
,

\langle
NTypeConst
\rangle
and
\langle
CTypeConst
\rangle
represent literals for values of types

\mathbb{N}
,
NType.t
and
CType.t
respectively.
\langle
MemBlockConst
\rangle

represents constant memory blocks.
\langle
CTypeUnFun
\rangle
and
\langle
CTypeBinFun
\rangle

represent unary and binary functions on
CType.t
.
\langle
NTypeBinFun
\rangle

represents binary functions on
NType.t
.
\langle
NExpr
\rangle
and
\langle
AExpr
\rangle

represent syntax for expressions which evaluate to
NType.t
and
CType.t
.
\langle
PExpr
\rangle
represents pointers to memory blocks that can be used to
write to memory.
\langle
MExpr
\rangle
represents references to memory blocks that can be
used to read from memory.
\langle
MemRef
\rangle
represents pairs of memory references and
block sizes. Finally,
\langle
DSHOperator
\rangle
represents
DHCOL
operators.

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

literasls for natural numbers

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NTypeConst}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

literals for NType.t values

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

literals for CType.t values

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{MemBlockConstBase}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\texttt{:}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\texttt{:}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\texttt{,}\;\langle\textrm{MemBlockBase}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{MemBlockConst}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{\lx@text@lbrace\lx@text@rbrace}\;\mid\;\texttt{\lx@text@lbrace}\langle\textrm{MemBlockConstBase}\rangle\texttt{\lx@text@rbrace}

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NTypeBinFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{NDiv}\;\mid\;\texttt{NMod}\;\mid\;\texttt{NPlus}\;\mid\;\texttt{NMinus}\;\mid\;\texttt{NMult}\;\mid\;\texttt{NMin}\;\mid\;\texttt{NMax}

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeUnFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

AAbs

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeBinFun}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{APlus}\;\mid\;\texttt{AMinus}\;\mid\;\texttt{AMult}\;\mid\;\texttt{AMin}\;\mid\;\texttt{AMax}\;\mid\;\texttt{AZless}

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{NVar}\;\langle\textrm{Nat}\rangle\;\mid\;\texttt{NConst}\;\langle\textrm{NConst}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{NBinFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{PVar}\;\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{MExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{MPtrDeref}\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{MConst}\;\langle\textrm{MemBlockConst}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NTypeConst}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{AVar}\;\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\;\mid\;\texttt{AConst}\;\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{ANth}\;\langle\textrm{MExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{NExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeUnFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{CTypeBinFun}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{MemRef}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{(}\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\texttt{,}\;\langle\textrm{NExpr}\rangle\texttt{)}

\displaystyle\langle\textrm{DSHOperator}\rangle

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHNop}\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHAssign}\;\langle\textrm{MemRef}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{MemRef}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHIMap}\;\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHBinOp}\;\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHMemMap2}\;\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\;

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHPower}\;\langle\textrm{NExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{MemRef}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{MemRef}\rangle\;

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\textrm{AExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHLoop}\;\langle\textrm{NatConst}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{DSHOperator}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHAlloc}\;\langle\textrm{NTypeConst}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{DSHOperator}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHMemInit}\;\langle\textrm{PExpr}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{CTypeConst}\rangle\;\mid\;

\displaystyle\texttt{DSHSeq}\;\langle\textrm{DSHOperator}\rangle\;\langle\textrm{DSHOperator}\rangle

D.2.
DHCOL
operators

In this section, we present detailed description of all
DHCOL
operators.
DHCOL
operators are deeply embedded in Coq: they are
represented as constructors for the ‘DSHOperator‘ inductive type.

DSHNop

This is a “no-op” operator. It does nothing.

DSHAssign

(
src

dst
:

MemRef
)

An assignment operator copies a single
CType.t
value from memory
location
src
to memory location
dst
.

DSHIMap

(
n
:

\mathbb{N}
)

(
x

y
:

PExpr
)

(
f
:

AExpr
)

It iterates the index from
0
to
n-1
. For each iteration, the
values from
x
at the index offset are added to the evaluation
context, and the expression
f
is evaluated. It could be viewed
as calling function
f
with the two arguments: an index
i
of type

NType.t
and a value
x[i]
of type
CType.t
. The result
is written to
y[i]
.

DSHBinOp

(
n
:

\mathbb{N}
)

(
x

y
:

PExpr
)

(
f
:

AExpr
)

This operator is similar to
DSHIMap
. It also iterates from
0

to
n-1
. For each iteration, it reads two values: at the index offset
and at
n
plus the index offset from
x
and adds them to the
evaluation context and then evaluates the expression
f
. It could
be viewed as calling the function
f
with the two arguments:
x[i]

and
x[n+i]
. The result is written to
y[i]
.

DSHMemMap2

(
n
:

\mathbb{N}
)

(
x0

x1

y
:

PExpr
)

(
f
:

AExpr
)

This is another iterative map. It also iterates from
0
to
n-1
. For
each iteration, it reads
x0[i]
and
x1[i]
and adds them to the
evaluation context and evaluates the expression
f
. It could be
viewed as calling the function
f
with the two arguments:
x0[i]
and

x1[i]
. The result is written to
y[i]
.

DSHPower

(
n
:
NExpr
)

(
src

dst
:

MemRef
)

(
f
:

AExpr
)

(
initial
:

CT
.
t
)

First, it initializes a memory location pointed by
dst
with

initial
value and proceeds to iterate
n
times. On each
iteration, values are loaded from
src
and
dst
, the

f
is evaluated, and the result of the evaluation is stored
back in
dst
. In C-like pseudo code, it could be expressed as:

*dst=initial; while(n--){*dst=f(*src,*dst)}
.

DSHLoop

(
n
:
\mathbb{N}
)

(
body
:

DSHOperator
)

This is a simple loop. It evaluates a body
n
times. For each
iteration, the value of the loop index (starting from
0
) is put on
the top of the evaluation context before evaluating the
body
.

DSHAlloc

(
size
:
NT
.
t
)

(
body
:

DSHOperator
)

Lexically scoped memory allocation. It allocates a new memory block of
size
size
. The newly allocated block is uninitialized. The new
block’s offset in memory is put on the top of the evaluation context
and then the
body
is evaluated. The block is only available
while the body is being evaluated.

DSHMemInit

(
y
:

PExpr
)

(
value
:

CT
.
t
)

Initialize all
elements of block
y
with given
value
.

DSHSeq

(
f

g
:

DSHOperator
)

Evaluate
f
and then, evaluate
g
. Memory modifications performed by
the evaluation of the first operator are visible during the evaluation
of the second.

D.3.
DHCOL
semantics

In this section, we present Big-Step Operational Semantics for the

DHCOL
language, informally described in Section
5
. This
semantics is implemented as a fixpoint evaluator, described in
Section
5.3
. In presenting
DHCOL
semantics, we will
use the following conventions:

•

lower-case names with subscript
s
will range over
string

•

lower-case names with subscript
N
will range over natural
numbers in
\mathbb{N}

•

lower-case names with subscript
i
will range over unsigned
fixed-length machine integers in
NType.t

•

lower-case names with subscript
c
will range over abstract

carrier type
values in
CTtype.t

•

lower-case names with subscript
b
will range over memory
blocks in
mem_block

•

lower-case names with subscript
n
will range over
NExpr

•

lower-case names with subscript
p
will range over
PExpr

•

lower-case names with subscript
m
will range over
MExpr

•

lower-case names with subscript
a
will range over
AExpr

•

upper-case names without subscript will range over
DSHOperator

D.3.1.
The set of states

The set of states has type

\mathrm{evalContext}\times\mathrm{memory}
, usually denoted as

(\sigma,m)
. The
evalContext
is a list of variable types and
bindings of type
DSHVal
:

⬇

DSHVal

:=

DSHn
atVal

\mathrm{value}_{i}

|

DSHCTypeVal

\mathrm{value}_{c}

|

DSHPtrVal

\mathrm{value}_{\mathbb{N}}

\mathrm{size}_{i}

We use square bracket notation
\sigma[\mathrm{index}_{\mathbb{N}}]
to reference a variable with the de Bruijn index
\mathrm{index}_{\mathbb{N}}
in the evaluation context
\sigma
. The type of
\sigma[\mathrm{index}_{\mathbb{N}}]
is
err DSHVal
.
It will be evaluated to
inr value
in case of successful lookup or
inl _
if there is no variable with such an index in
\sigma
.

The
memory
represents the state of the memory, per the memory model
described in Section
4.1.1
.
We also use square bracket notation
m[\mathrm{addr}_{\mathbb{N}}]
to reference
a memory block at address
\mathrm{addr}_{\mathbb{N}}
. Thus, the type of
m[\mathrm{addr}_{\mathbb{N}}]
is

err mem_block
. It will be evaluated to
inr

\mathrm{value}_{b}
in case of successful lookup
or
inl _
if the address is out of range.

Finally, we write
block_{b}[\mathrm{offset}_{\mathbb{N}}]
to access a value
at offset
\mathrm{offset}_{\mathbb{N}}
in memory block
block_{b}
. The type of
such expression is
err CType.t
. It will be evaluated to
inr

\mathrm{value}_{c}
in case of successful lookup or
inl _
if the offset is out of range.

D.3.2.
Expressions

Expressions with types
PExpr
,
MExpr
,
NExpr
, and

AExpr
are evaluated in a given state
(\sigma,m)
to their
respective values of type
(
\mathbb{N}
\times
NT
.
t
)
,

(
mem_block
\times
NT
.
t
)
,
NT.t
, and
CT.t
. The evaluation can fail, so the
err
monad is used to
wrap the evaluation results. The relation between an expression together with the state and the evaluation result is denoted as
\Downarrow
.

PExpr
evaluation semantics

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{PVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(v_{\mathbb{N}},\mathrm{size}_{i})\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;(\mathrm{DSHPtrVal}\;v_{\mathbb{N}}\;\mathrm{size}_{i})

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{PVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"error looking up PVar"}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

MExpr
evaluation semantics

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{MPtrDeref}\;x_{p},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(v_{b},\mathrm{size}_{i})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle x_{p},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(x_{\mathbb{N}},\mathrm{size}_{i})m[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;v_{b}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{MPtrDeref}\;x_{p},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle x_{p},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{MPtrDeref}\;x_{p},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"MPtrDeref lookup failed"}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle x_{p},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(x_{\mathbb{N}},\mathrm{size}_{i})m[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{MConst}\;v_{b}\;\mathrm{size}_{i},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(v_{b},\mathrm{size}_{i})

NExpr
evaluation semantics

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;v_{i}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;(\mathrm{DSHnatVal}\;v_{i})

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"invalid NVar type"}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;(\mathrm{DSHCTypeVal}\;v_{c})

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"invalid NVar type"}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;(\mathrm{DSHPtrVal}\;v_{\mathbb{N}}\;\mathrm{size}_{i})

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NConst}\;v_{i},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;v_{i}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NDiv}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;\frac{a_{i}}{b_{i}}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}b_{i}\not=0

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NDiv}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NDiv}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"Division by 0"}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}b_{i}=0

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NDiv}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}b_{i}\not=0\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMod}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(a_{i}\bmod b_{i})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}b_{i}\not=0

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMod}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMod}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"Mod by 0"}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}b_{i}=0

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMod}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}b_{i}\not=0\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NPlus}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(a_{i}+b_{i})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NPlus}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NPlus}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMinus}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(a_{i}-b_{i})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMinus}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMinus}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMult}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(a_{i}\cdot b_{i})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMult}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMult}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMin}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;\min(a_{i},b_{i})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMin}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMin}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMax}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;\max(a_{i},b_{i})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{i}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMax}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{NMax}\;a_{n}\;b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{i}\langle b_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

AExpr
evaluation semantics

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;v_{c}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;(\mathrm{DSHCTypeVal}\;v_{c})

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"invalid AVar type"}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;(\mathrm{DSHnatVal}\;v_{i})

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AVar}\;x_{\mathbb{N}},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"invalid AVar type"}\sigma[x_{\mathbb{N}}]=\mathrm{inr}\;(\mathrm{DSHPtrVal}\;v_{\mathbb{N}}\;\mathrm{size}_{i})

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AConst}\;v_{c},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;v_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AAbs}\;a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;|v_{c}|\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;v_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AAbs}\;a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{APlus}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(a_{c}+b_{c})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{APlus}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{APlus}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMinus}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(a_{c}-b_{c})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMinus}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMinus}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMult}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(a_{c}\cdot b_{c})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMult}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMult}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMin}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;\min(a_{c},b_{c})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMin}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMin}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMax}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;\max(a_{c},b_{c})\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMax}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AMax}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AZless}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;\mathrm{one}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{c}a_{c}<b_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AZless}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;\mathrm{zero}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;b_{c}a_{c}\geq b_{c}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AZless}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{AZless}\;a_{a}\;b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle a_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;a_{c}\langle b_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{ANth}\;x_{m}\;i_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\langle i_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{ANth}\;x_{m}\;i_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle i_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;i_{i}\langle x_{m},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{ANth}\;x_{m}\;i_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{"ANth index out of bounds"}\lx@proof@logical@and\langle i_{n},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;i_{i}\langle x_{m},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(x_{b},\mathrm{size}_{i})i_{i}\geq\mathrm{size}_{i}

D.3.3.
Operators

Operators evaluate in a given state
(\sigma,m)
, and the result of the
evaluation is either an error signaled via an
err
monad by
returning
(\mathrm{inl}\;error\_message)
or the updated memory
m^{\prime}

returned as
(\mathrm{inr}\;m^{\prime})
.

We write
m[\mathrm{addr}_{\mathbb{N}}/\mathrm{value}_{b}]
for the memory
state obtained by replacing a memory block at address
\mathrm{addr}_{\mathbb{N}}

with the new memory block
\mathrm{value}_{b}
. To free a memory at
address
\mathrm{addr}_{\mathbb{N}}
and designate this address as currently unassigned
(which would lead to an error trying to access it) we write
m[\mathrm{addr}_{\mathbb{N}}/]
.

Similarly,
\mathrm{block}_{b}[\mathrm{offset}_{\mathbb{N}}/\mathrm{value}_{c}]

will result in a memory block with value at
\mathrm{offset}_{\mathbb{N}}

replaced with
\mathrm{value}_{c}
. Notation

\mathrm{block}_{b}[0\ldots\mathrm{offset}_{\mathbb{N}}/\mathrm{value}_{c}]
means
to initialize memory block elements with offsets in the range

[0\ldots\mathrm{offset}_{n})
with value
\mathrm{value}_{c}
.

We use

\mathrm{from\_nat}:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathrm{err\;NT.t}

function to convert natural numbers to
NT.t
. Such a conversion
could return an error because the set of natural numbers is infinite,
while
NT.t
is a finite set. The conversion in the other direction:
from
NT.t
to
\mathbb{N}
is implicit, and we can use just
NT.t

where
\mathbb{N}
is expected.

We use the
cons
operator
::
to prepend
\sigma
with a new element which will have index
0
. For example:

(\mathrm{DSHnatVal}\;n_{i})::\sigma
.

Below, we present big-step inference rules for
DHCOL
operators. For
brevity, we include only rules describing successful evaluation and
omit rules describing error handling. Since error handling is
always done via the
err
monad, whenever the premise of a rule contains a
clause in the form
x=\mathrm{inr}\;y
, it should be assumed that
there is a corresponding rule for the error case
x=\mathrm{inl}\;\mathrm{msg}_{s}
with
\mathrm{inl}\;\textit{error\_message}
on the
right side of the
\Downarrow
in the conclusion.

\displaystyle{\langle\mathrm{DSHNop},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;m}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{evalIMap}\;0\;x_{b}\;y_{b}\;f_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;y_{b}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{evalMap2}\;0\;x0_{b}\;x1_{b}\;y_{b}\;f_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;y_{b}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{evalBinOp}\;0\;\mathrm{off}_{i}\;x_{b}\;y_{b}\;f_{a},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;y_{b}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{evalPower}\;0\;x_{b}\;y_{b}\;\mathrm{src}_{i}\;\mathrm{dst}_{i}\;f_{a}\,\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;y_{b}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{DSHLoop}\;0\;\mathrm{body},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;m

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{DSHSeq}\;f\;g,\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;m^{\prime\prime}\lx@proof@logical@and{\langle f,\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;m^{\prime}}{\langle g,\sigma,m^{\prime}\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;m^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{DSHMemInit}\;y_{p}\;v_{c},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;m[y_{\mathbb{N}}/(y_{b}[0\ldots\mathrm{ysize}_{i}/v_{c}])]\lx@proof@logical@and{\langle y_{p},\sigma,m\rangle\Downarrow\mathrm{inr}\;(y_{\mathbb{N}},\mathrm{ysize}_{i})}{y_{b}=\mathrm{inr}\;m[y_{\mathbb{N}}]}
